<div class="part-divider"><div class="pn">Part XII</div><h1>Reference</h1><p>This Part is the primer's back matter: a glossary of every term the registry assigns, the chain map with the Parts that carry each box, the margin pool in one table, an index of every company and institution named, the full register of what the research could not find, and the consolidated list of sources. A reader who has a term, a company or a figure in mind can start here and be sent to the section that treats it.</p></div>

### How this Part is built

Part XII contains almost no new prose. Its six sections are compiled from files that already exist: the term registry (research/term-registry.md), the chain map drawn for the orchestrator, the margin exhibits of Part XI §86, the eleven Part files themselves, and section D of the reconciliation (research/reconciliation.md). Small scripts did the compiling, so that each entry can be traced to the file it came from and the compilation can be re-run when a Part changes. Where this Part states a figure, it is a figure another Part already states, with that Part named; where it states a definition, it is the definition the owner Part boxed. Nothing here is ranked and nothing is a view. Cross-references use the outline's section numbers, so "Part VII §46" means section 46 of the primer, which sits in Part VII.

## 90. Glossary

The glossary lists every term in section 1 of the term registry, 302 entries in all, in alphabetical order. The registry sorted terms under the Part that owns them; this section sorts them by name and gives the owner at the end of each entry. Each entry has the term, its acronym or alias where the registry records one, a definition of one or two sentences, and an owner line of the form "Owner: Part N §M", which names the section where the owner Part boxes the term. Where the owner Part did not box the term but another Part did (because that Part used it first), the owner line names both: "Owner: Part VIII; boxed in Part II §17". Where no Part boxed the term, the owner Part alone is given.

The definitions are pared, not rewritten. For the terms a Part boxed, the definition is the first one or two sentences of that Part's box, with citations and cross-reference sentences removed. For the dozen terms no Part boxed (company and timeline entries, aliases, and a few labels), the definition is the registry's own, tightened to two sentences. Figures that appear in a definition are the owner Part's figures and carry that Part's sources; the owner section should be read for the basis and date of any number quoted here. Twenty-four terms are defined differently by two or more dossiers; the registry's section 3 quotes both definitions and names the form the primer uses, and the owner Parts flag the difference where it matters (for example "core", which means the hot module in Part I and the returned unserviceable unit in Part VII §47). The Spine's own vocabulary is the registry's section 4, forty grouped entries, and every one of them appears below under its owner.

<dl>
<dt>2025 Partnership (2025 SPV; FTAI SCI I; inaugural Strategic Capital vehicle)</dt>
<dd>The first SCI vehicle, launched 30 December 2024: a closed-end partnership whose asset-owning special-purpose vehicles buy on-lease mid-life 737NG and A320ceo aircraft. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §61.</span></dd>
<dt>2026 SPV (SCI II; second investment vehicle)</dt>
<dd>The second SCI vehicle. Its $2.0B warehouse facility from 13 lenders, with a $1.0B accordion to $3.0B, closed on 14 August 2026; it buys on-lease mid-life 737NG and A320ceo aircraft from August 2026, and its first disclosed deal is 17 737-700s bought on a sale-leaseback with WestJet (§67). <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §62.</span></dd>
<dt>25% share goal</dt>
<dd>FTAI's stated goal of 25% of CFM56 shop visits, which the 3,000-module capacity is said to support alongside 100 Mod-1 units a year. The denominator (the industry's annual shop-visit count, or a module equivalent of it) and the target year were not disclosed in anything the research retrieved. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VII §49.</span></dd>
<dt>737NG / A320ceo (737 Next Generation; A320 current engine option)</dt>
<dd>The 737NG (Next Generation) is the Boeing 737-600, -700, -800 and -900ER, built from about 1997 to 2019 and powered only by the CFM56-7B. The A320ceo (current engine option) is the Airbus A318, A319, A320 and A321 family built from about 1988 to 2020, powered by either the CFM56-5B or the V2500 (the alternative engine from the IAE consortium, §10). <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §2.</span></dd>
<dt>Accessory gearbox (AGB; accessory drive)</dt>
<dd>The accessory gearbox is a train of gears driven off the high-pressure spool by a radial shaft running out through the fan frame. It turns the fuel pump, the oil pumps, the hydraulic pump and the aircraft's electrical generator. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §3.</span></dd>
<dt>Accordion</dt>
<dd>A pre-agreed option in a credit agreement to enlarge the facility, exercisable if lenders agree to fund the increase; the borrower is not entitled to the extra amount until they do. The 2026 warehouse is $2.0B of committed capacity with a $1.0B accordion, so up to $3.0B. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §62.</span></dd>
<dt>Adjusted EBIT</dt>
<dd>Adjusted EBIT is operating profit after depreciation and amortisation, before purchase-price-allocation effects and other adjustments each company defines. MTU Aero Engines reports its commercial maintenance business on this basis (€478M on €6.0B in 2025, 8.0%), and Lufthansa Technik reports its whole company on it (€603M on €8.049B, 7.5%). <span class="cite">Owner: Part XI §86.</span></dd>
<dt>Adjusted EBITDA (consolidated; the definition) (EBITDA)</dt>
<dd>EBITDA is earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation and amortization. Adjusted EBITDA is FTAI's company-defined variant. <span class="cite">Owner: Part X §80.</span></dd>
<dt>Advance rate / borrowing base</dt>
<dd>The advance rate is the percentage of each asset's value that the banks will lend against it under a warehouse facility. The borrowing base is the total credit available at any moment, recomputed as assets are added, sold or revalued: the sum over the assets of value times advance rate. <span class="cite">Owner: Part II §17.</span></dd>
<dt>Aeroderivative</dt>
<dd>An aeroderivative is an aircraft engine adapted to drive a shaft for stationary use. The fan and bypass duct are removed and extra compressor stages replace the fan; the exhaust nozzle is replaced by a power turbine, or the engine's own low-pressure turbine is used; a gearbox and a generator are fitted; and the kerosene fuel system is changed for gas. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §68.</span></dd>
<dt>Aerospace products revenue (income-statement line)</dt>
<dd>The consolidated income-statement line for the segment's sales of modules, engines and parts to customers other than the Strategic Capital vehicles: $454,970 thousand in 2023, $1,079,821 thousand in 2024 and $1,600,456 thousand in 2025. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VII §47.</span></dd>
<dt>Aerospace Products segment (AP)</dt>
<dd>FTAI's reportable segment that develops, repairs and sells CFM56 and V2500 engines, modules and parts through the Module Factory; FY2025 segment revenue $1,936.2M and segment Adjusted EBITDA (FTAI's adjusted earnings measure) $671.3M; Adjusted EBITDA is earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation and amortisation as the company adjusts them, defined in Part X §80. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VII; boxed in Part I §11.</span></dd>
<dt>Aftermarket demand</dt>
<dd>Aftermarket demand for an engine type is the sum of the work and assets needed to keep that type flying: shop visits, modules, spare engines, used serviceable material and leases of engines and aircraft. It is generated by the installed base multiplied by how hard that base flies, net of the engines that leave. <span class="cite">Owner: Part III §18.</span></dd>
<dt>Air permit</dt>
<dd>An air permit is the authorisation, issued under the US Clean Air Act by the state or county agency that implements it, to construct and operate a source of air emissions. A stationary source needs a construction and operating permit before it runs, and the permit sets the emission limits and controls the source must meet. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §73.</span></dd>
<dt>Aircraft on ground (AOG)</dt>
<dd>An aircraft on ground is one that cannot fly for want of a serviceable part or engine. Short-term engine leases exist to cover AOG events and planned shop visits; a lessor that can deliver an engine within days is selling the airline the difference between a grounded aircraft and a flying one. <span class="cite">Owner: Part II §14.</span></dd>
<dt>Airworthiness Directive / Service Bulletin (AD / SB)</dt>
<dd>A Service Bulletin is a manufacturer's instruction to modify or inspect; an Airworthiness Directive is a regulator's order making a fix or inspection mandatory. Compliance records for both must travel with life-limited and hard-time parts. <span class="cite">Owner: Part V; boxed in Part I §3.</span></dd>
<dt>Airworthiness Limitations Section / Instructions for Continued Airworthiness (ALS / ICA)</dt>
<dd>The Instructions for Continued Airworthiness are the manufacturer's approved maintenance instructions for a type-certificated product, attached to the type certificate: the Engine Shop Manual and the component maintenance manuals (Part I §9) are part of them. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VI §38.</span></dd>
<dt>Anchor investor</dt>
<dd>An early, large limited partner whose commitment seeds a fund-raise and signals to later investors that the terms have been negotiated by someone with scale. The February 2026 results release said fundraising for the second vehicle had launched "with anchor investor commitments"; the anchors' identities and amounts were not disclosed. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §62.</span></dd>
<dt>Anticipated repayment date / legal final maturity (ARD)</dt>
<dd>The anticipated repayment date is the date after which a securitization sweeps all excess cash to pay down principal faster and steps up the coupon on the notes, so that the deal is designed to be refinanced or called at that date. The legal final maturity is the much later last date by which the notes must be repaid. <span class="cite">Owner: Part II §17.</span></dd>
<dt>Appraiser (IBA; mba Aviation; Ascend by Cirium; AVITAS)</dt>
<dd>An appraiser is an independent valuer that publishes base and market values and lease rates for aircraft and engines, at half-life unless stated; the four this primer cites are IBA, mba Aviation, Ascend by Cirium and AVITAS (a US appraisal firm). The market trades on their figures, and securitisation rating agencies test deals against them. <span class="cite">Owner: Part II; boxed in Part I §8.</span></dd>
<dt>ASC 606 / noncash consideration</dt>
<dd>ASC 606, Topic 606 of the US Accounting Standards Codification (ASC), is the US revenue-recognition standard for contracts with customers. Under it, noncash consideration received from a customer is measured at fair value and included in the transaction price. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VII §47.</span></dd>
<dt>ASC 610-20</dt>
<dd>Topic 610-20 of the US Accounting Standards Codification (ASC), the standard for gains and losses on the derecognition of nonfinancial assets that are not part of a company's ordinary revenue-generating activities. FTAI books aircraft sales to the 2025 Partnership under it, describing them as "non-recurring in nature and not considered part of the Company's ordinary activities". <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §64.</span></dd>
<dt>Asset-backed securitization (ABS)</dt>
<dd>An asset-backed securitization is a transaction in which a lessor sells a portfolio of leased aircraft or engines to a bankruptcy-remote SPV, which issues notes to investors secured on the assets and on their lease cash flows. The notes are divided into tranches paid in order of seniority. <span class="cite">Owner: Part II §17.</span></dd>
<dt>Asset-level debt (Non-recourse debt)</dt>
<dd>Borrowing by the asset-owning SPVs, secured on the aircraft and their leases, with no recourse to the sponsor. If the aircraft and their rent do not cover the loan, the lenders' claim stops at the SPV; FTAI's own balance sheet is not exposed. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §61.</span></dd>
<dt>Asset-light model</dt>
<dd>Management's term for owning fewer leased assets directly and earning instead servicing fees, maintenance work and equity income from vehicles that own them. The Strategic Capital releases say the initiative lets FTAI "maintain an asset-light business model", and the July 2026 cut to Aviation Leasing guidance was explained as "reflecting a continued shift to an asset-light business model". <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §60.</span></dd>
<dt>Audit Committee / Audit Committee review</dt>
<dd>The board committee of independent directors that oversees financial reporting and the external auditor. On 18 January 2025 FTAI's Audit Committee "determined to begin a review, which included engaging independent advisors, in response to the assertions alleged in the January 15, 2025 report issued by Muddy Waters Research". <span class="cite">Owner: Part VII §54.</span></dd>
<dt>Auxiliary power unit (APU)</dt>
<dd>The auxiliary power unit is the small gas turbine in the aircraft's tail that supplies electrical power and compressed air on the ground and in some flight phases. It is one of the highest-value airframe rotables in a part-out and one of the components for which lessors collect maintenance reserves (Part II §13). <span class="cite">Owner: Part V §32.</span></dd>
<dt>Average age</dt>
<dd>The average age of a fleet is the mean number of years since delivery of its aircraft. Maintenance need rises with age in steps rather than smoothly: the first shop visit, the first life-limited-part replacement, the first heavy airframe check. <span class="cite">Owner: Part III §18.</span></dd>
<dt>Aviation Leasing segment (AL segment)</dt>
<dd>FTAI's reportable segment that owns, leases and sells aircraft and engines, directly and through an equity-method investment; 290 assets at 31 Dec 2025 and segment Adjusted EBITDA $608.9M for FY2025. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII; boxed in Part I §11.</span></dd>
<dt>Backlog</dt>
<dd>Backlog is the count of firm orders not yet delivered. Airbus's stood at a record 8,754 aircraft at the end of December 2025, more than ten years of production at 2026 delivery rates; the 737 MAX backlog exceeds 4,800 aircraft. <span class="cite">Owner: Part III §21.</span></dd>
<dt>Back-to-birth traceability (BTB)</dt>
<dd>Back-to-birth traceability is a continuous record, for each life-limited part, of every cycle it has flown on every engine since manufacture, with no gaps. A gap means the part's remaining life cannot be proven and it is treated as unusable. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §9.</span></dd>
<dt>Balance of plant / packaging</dt>
<dd>The balance of plant is everything around the turbine that a stationary power unit needs: the enclosure, the trailer or skid, the generator and gearbox, the air-inlet filter house, the exhaust and silencing, the gas fuel skid, lubrication and cooling, fire suppression and the control system. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §68.</span></dd>
<dt>Base value</dt>
<dd>Base value is an appraiser's opinion of an asset's underlying economic value in an open, unrestricted, stable market with balanced supply and demand. Market value is the appraiser's opinion of the most likely trading price in the actual current market between willing, informed parties; it sits above base value in a tight market and below it in a soft one. <span class="cite">Owner: Part II; boxed in Part I §8.</span></dd>
<dt>Basic / diluted EPS; RSU / PSU</dt>
<dd>Basic earnings per share divides net income attributable to ordinary shareholders by the weighted-average number of ordinary shares outstanding in the period. Diluted earnings per share adds the shares that would be issued if outstanding restricted stock units (RSUs, which vest with time) and performance stock units (PSUs, which vest on performance conditions) were settled. <span class="cite">Owner: Part X §83.</span></dd>
<dt>Behind-the-meter (BTM)</dt>
<dd>Behind-the-meter generation is electricity generated on the customer's side of the utility meter, at the customer's site, so that it never passes through the grid. Enverus estimates that about 40% of installed data-center capacity is behind the meter, that this adds about 1.3 Bcf/d of incremental gas demand by 2030, and that half of it sits in ERCOT and PJM. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §71.</span></dd>
<dt>Beyond economic repair (BER)</dt>
<dd>A removed part is beyond economic repair when it is outside limits and not worth repairing: the repair would cost more than the part would fetch, or no approved repair exists. It is scrapped. <span class="cite">Owner: Part V §31.</span></dd>
<dt>Blackbird / Thunderbolt (Air Lease)</dt>
<dd>Air Lease Corporation's two routes to third-party capital. Blackbird Capital I and II are investment funds whose aircraft Air Lease manages and in which it holds non-controlling interests of 9.5% of the equity of each. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §65.</span></dd>
<dt>Booster (Low-pressure compressor (LPC))</dt>
<dd>The booster is a small three-stage compressor mounted directly behind the fan on the low-pressure shaft. It gives the air bound for the core a first squeeze before the main compressor takes over. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §3.</span></dd>
<dt>Borescope / boroblend</dt>
<dd>A borescope inspection looks inside the engine with a camera passed through ports in the casing, on wing or in a shop. A boroblend repair grinds out small blade damage found that way, through the same ports, without disassembly. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §6.</span></dd>
<dt>Bridging power</dt>
<dd>Bridging power is temporary on-site generation used until a grid interconnection is available. ProEnergy's customers describe their turbine plants as "bridging power for five to seven years, which is when they expect to have grid interconnection". <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §73.</span></dd>
<dt>Build standard (Configuration)</dt>
<dd>The build standard is the set of service-bulletin states a module has been built to. The manufacturer issues service bulletins that change hardware over the years, and two modules at different standards may not mate, or may not be approved to run together. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §3.</span></dd>
<dt>Bypass / turbofan / high-bypass turbofan</dt>
<dd>A turbofan is a jet engine in which a large front fan pushes most of the incoming air around the hot core rather than through it. The air that goes around is the bypass stream. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §1.</span></dd>
<dt>Capacity factor</dt>
<dd>The capacity factor is a plant's actual output over a period divided by what it would have produced running at full load for the whole period. A unit that ran flat out all year would have a factor of 100%; the fuel-burn example below uses 90% as an assumption for a baseload data-center unit. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §68.</span></dd>
<dt>Capacity (shop) (Inductions per year)</dt>
<dd>A shop's capacity is the number of engine inductions a year it can take. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IV §25.</span></dd>
<dt>Carlyle Aviation Partners (fee comparable)</dt>
<dd>The aviation arm of The Carlyle Group, which runs closed-end funds that earn carried interest and also sponsors aircraft securitizations that it services. Carlyle's FY2025 10-K states that servicing fees on its aviation securitizations are "generally in the range of 2% of rents, incentive fees up to 5% of rents in the aggregate, and 3% of sales proceeds". <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §65.</span></dd>
<dt>Catalogue list price (CLP; list price; catalog price)</dt>
<dd>The catalogue list price is the manufacturer's published price for every new spare part, repriced at least annually. A shop passes it through at list plus a handling margin, and used and alternative parts are priced as discounts to it. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IV; boxed in Part I §4.</span></dd>
<dt>Cayman Islands exempted company / redomiciliation (Redomicile)</dt>
<dd>A Cayman Islands exempted company is a company incorporated in the Cayman Islands that conducts its business mainly outside the islands; it is a common holding-company form for aircraft lessors. Redomiciliation is the move of a company's legal home from one jurisdiction to another. <span class="cite">Owner: Part X §77.</span></dd>
<dt>Center of Excellence (CoE)</dt>
<dd>Center of Excellence is StandardAero's term for a shop dedicated to a single engine platform. <span class="cite">Owner: Part XI §87.</span></dd>
<dt>CFM International (CFM)</dt>
<dd>CFM International is the joint venture of GE Aerospace and Safran Aircraft Engines (formerly Snecma) that is the OEM and type-certificate holder of the CFM56 and of its successor, the LEAP (CFM's new-generation engine, treated in §10). <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §2.</span></dd>
<dt>CFM materials agreement</dt>
<dd>A multi-year agreement between FTAI and CFM International announced on 22 January 2026 under which, in the release's words, FTAI "secures OEM replacement part supply, thrust performance upgrades and component repair" for CFM56 engines. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VII §52.</span></dd>
<dt>CFM56 family (-3, -5A, -5B, -5C, -7B) (CFM56-7B; CFM56-5B)</dt>
<dd>The CFM56 is a two-shaft, high-bypass turbofan of which more than 35,000 have been delivered since entry into service in 1982, with roughly 23,000 to 24,000 in service depending on the source (§2.2). The -7B is the sole engine of the 737NG, with more than 15,000 built and the last engine for a commercial 737NG delivered in 2019. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §2.</span></dd>
<dt>Channel stuffing</dt>
<dd>Recognising sales to intermediaries or related parties ahead of end demand, so that reported revenue runs ahead of what final customers have bought. It appears among the allegations as third parties summarised the report; the counterparty the report named was not retrieved. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VII §54.</span></dd>
<dt>Chromalloy</dt>
<dd>Chromalloy is a Palm Beach Gardens, Florida, company that repairs and coats turbine-engine parts, makes investment castings (a casting method in which a ceramic mould is formed around a wax pattern of the part), and designs and manufactures PMA parts. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VI §41.</span></dd>
<dt>Classified board</dt>
<dd>A classified board is one whose directors serve staggered terms, so that only a portion of the board stands for election in any one year. FTAI's seven directors serve three-year terms in three classes, with two or three terms expiring each year. <span class="cite">Owner: Part X §78.</span></dd>
<dt>Closed-end vehicle</dt>
<dd>A fund that raises a fixed amount of equity commitments up to a hard cap, invests them during a deployment period, then stops buying and enters a harvest phase in which it collects rent, distributes cash to its investors and sells assets until it winds up. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §61.</span></dd>
<dt>Combustor (Combustion chamber)</dt>
<dd>The combustor is the chamber between the high-pressure compressor and the high-pressure turbine in which fuel is sprayed into the compressed air and burned. It is part of the core module. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §3.</span></dd>
<dt>Commercial Engines &amp; Services (CES)</dt>
<dd>Commercial Engines &amp; Services is the GE Aerospace segment that sells new commercial engines and spare parts and performs shop visits, in GE's own shops and under long-term service agreements. In 2025 it reported revenue of $33,314 million, of which $25,010 million (75.1%) was services, and segment profit of $8,861 million, a 26.6% margin. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IV §25.</span></dd>
<dt>Commissioning</dt>
<dd>Commissioning is the on-site testing and hand-over of installed equipment: the unit is connected to gas and to the customer's switchgear, run, and shown to meet its specification before the customer accepts it. It is the last milestone payment under the J&amp;F order. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §70.</span></dd>
<dt>Comparable metrics</dt>
<dd>Comparable metrics are the figures from a peer's filing that can be set beside a figure in FTAI's filings because both measure the same physical or financial quantity: owned engines, utilisation on a stated definition, maintenance-reserve revenue, gain on sale of leased equipment, a segment margin on a stated basis, approvals of alternative parts per year, gross margin on used material, and the services share of a manufacturer's revenue. <span class="cite">Owner: Part XI §86.</span></dd>
<dt>Component Maintenance Manual (CMM)</dt>
<dd>The Component Maintenance Manual is the manufacturer's document that sets inspection and repair limits for an individual component; the Engine Shop Manual is the engine-level equivalent. <span class="cite">Owner: Part V; boxed in Part I §9.</span></dd>
<dt>Conduct Policies (CFM–IATA, 2018)</dt>
<dd>The Conduct Policies are the commitments CFM International published in 2018 to settle a complaint the International Air Transport Association (IATA) had brought to the European Commission about CFM's aftermarket conduct. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VI §42.</span></dd>
<dt>Consignment</dt>
<dd>Consignment is teardown and sale of parts on the owner's behalf for a fee or a commission, with title to the parts retained by the owner, who may be an airline, a lessor, a bank or an insurer. It is the alternative to buying feedstock outright and bearing the inventory risk. <span class="cite">Owner: Part V §32.</span></dd>
<dt>Core (Gas generator)</dt>
<dd>The core is the hot, fast, expensive middle of the engine: the high-pressure compressor, the combustor and the high-pressure turbine, treated as one module. The industry also calls it the gas generator. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §1.</span></dd>
<dt>Cost build (shop visit)</dt>
<dd>The cost of a shop visit built up as the sum of labour, new OEM parts, component repairs, life-limited-part replacement (new or used) and the test-cell run, plus freight, consumables and the shop's margin. Material is about 60 to 70% of the total; Part I §7 works the example. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I.</span></dd>
<dt>Cost of sales (segment versus consolidated)</dt>
<dd>Two cost-of-sales figures for 2025 exist in the FY2025 10-K. One is $1,240,368 thousand, which the orchestrator's later reading identified as the Aerospace Products segment's cost of sales in the segment expenses table; one dossier had returned it against a segment query but could not confirm its scope. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VII §53.</span></dd>
<dt>Critical component / critical part</dt>
<dd>Under the FAA–EASA bilateral's Technical Implementation Procedures, a critical component is "a part identified as critical by the design approval holder during the product certification process or otherwise by the Authority for the State of Design. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VI §38.</span></dd>
<dt>Customer concentration</dt>
<dd>Customer concentration is the share of revenue that comes from a company's largest customers, which US filings must disclose for any customer above 10%. FTAI's Q3 2025 quarterly report says two customers in Aerospace Products accounted for 16% and 11% of revenue in the quarter. <span class="cite">Owner: Part X §78.</span></dd>
<dt>Cycles remaining (CR)</dt>
<dd>Cycles remaining is a life-limited part's certified limit minus the cycles it has consumed; used-part packages are advertised on it ("6235 CR"). Cycles since new is the number of cycles an engine or part has flown since manufacture. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §4.</span></dd>
<dt>Cycles since new (CSN)</dt>
<dd>Cycles remaining is a life-limited part's certified limit minus the cycles it has consumed; used-part packages are advertised on it ("6235 CR"). Cycles since new is the number of cycles an engine or part has flown since manufacture. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §4.</span></dd>
<dt>Deployment / fully committed / harvest phase</dt>
<dd>Deployment is the buying phase, during which commitments are called and aircraft acquired. "Fully committed" means every dollar of equity has been allocated to signed acquisitions, even if some closings are still pending; it is a different state from "fully deployed", which means closed and funded, and the releases use both words at different dates (§62.1). <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §61.</span></dd>
<dt>DER repair</dt>
<dd>A DER repair is a major repair whose data a Designated Engineering Representative approved on FAA Form 8110-3, rather than being taken from the manufacturer's shop manual. The approved data may be one-time, covering a specific serial-numbered part, or multiple-use, repeatable on any eligible part; a multiple-use approval is what turns a repair into a product a shop can sell. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VI §40.</span></dd>
<dt>Designated Engineering Representative (DER (company DER / consultant DER))</dt>
<dd>A Designated Engineering Representative is an individual engineer appointed by the FAA under 14 CFR Part 183 to examine engineering data and make findings of compliance on the FAA's behalf within a delegated technical area, such as structures, powerplant, engines or systems. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VI §40.</span></dd>
<dt>Discontinued operations</dt>
<dd>Discontinued operations is the accounting presentation under which a business that a company has disposed of, or is about to dispose of, is shown as a single net line below the results of the continuing business, with prior periods restated the same way. <span class="cite">Owner: Part X §77.</span></dd>
<dt>Disk / spool</dt>
<dd>A disk is the heavy wheel to which a row of blades attaches. A spool is several disks machined as a single piece. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §4.</span></dd>
<dt>Distribution (new parts)</dt>
<dd>Distribution is reselling new OEM parts under a distribution agreement: the distributor holds inventory and earns the margin between the manufacturer's transfer price and its sale price. Because the manufacturer sets the cost of goods, a distribution margin is structurally thinner than a PMA or used-material margin. <span class="cite">Owner: Part XI §86.</span></dd>
<dt>Dividend (ordinary) / share repurchase program</dt>
<dd>An ordinary dividend is the cash per share the board declares each quarter on the ordinary shares; it is discretionary and ranks behind the cumulative preferred dividends. A share repurchase program is a board authorisation to buy back up to a stated dollar amount of shares over time at management's discretion; it is not a commitment to buy any. <span class="cite">Owner: Part X §83.</span></dd>
<dt>Dual release</dt>
<dd>A dual release is one tag signed under both FAA and EASA authority, so that a part can move between US- and EU-registered aircraft without re-certification. The research reached industry descriptions only. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §9.</span></dd>
<dt>Durability Improvement Package / LEAP durability kit (DIP; HPT durability kit)</dt>
<dd>The durability kit is CFM's package of hardware changes to lengthen the LEAP's time on wing: a new high-pressure turbine stage-1 blade and nozzle and a forward inner nozzle support, "designed to more than double time on wing, especially in hot and harsh environments". The LEAP-1A kit is certified and "now incorporated into all deliveries and shop visits". <span class="cite">Owner: Part III; boxed in Part I §10.</span></dd>
<dt>EASA Form 1 (Form 1)</dt>
<dd>EASA Form 1 is the European agency's authorised release certificate, the equivalent of Form 8130-3, issued by an EASA-approved maintenance or production organisation. New parts made under a European production organisation approval (POA) "are typically ineligible for an 8130-3 tag and are more properly released on an EASA Form 1". <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §9.</span></dd>
<dt>EGT margin (EGTM)</dt>
<dd>EGT margin is "the difference between the maximum temperature limit on the engine and the actual temperature read at the time of take-off", measured at full take-off thrust on a hot day so that engines can be compared. It shrinks as the engine wears, fastest in the first 1,000 to 2,000 cycles after a shop visit. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §5.</span></dd>
<dt>Employees</dt>
<dd>FTAI reports 985 "full-time employees and independent contractors" at 31 December 2025, a single figure that mixes two categories, and separately 494 full-time employees in Canada, about 71% of whom are covered by collective bargaining agreements. The Canadian figure counts employees only, so the two numbers are not on one basis. <span class="cite">Owner: Part X §85.</span></dd>
<dt>End-of-lease compensation (EOL compensation)</dt>
<dd>End-of-lease compensation is the alternative regime, used for lessees whose credit is strong enough that the lessor does not demand monthly reserves. <span class="cite">Owner: Part II §12.</span></dd>
<dt>Engine flight cycle (EFC; FC; cycle)</dt>
<dd>An engine flight cycle (EFC), or simply a flight cycle (FC) or "cycle", is one take-off and landing. An engine flight hour (EFH), or flight hour (FH), is one hour of engine operation. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §1.</span></dd>
<dt>Engine flight hour (EFH; FH; flight hour)</dt>
<dd>An engine flight cycle (EFC), or simply a flight cycle (FC) or "cycle", is one take-off and landing. An engine flight hour (EFH), or flight hour (FH), is one hour of engine operation. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §1.</span></dd>
<dt>Engine lease: short-term / long-term</dt>
<dd>A short-term engine lease runs from a few months to about three years and covers a shop visit or an aircraft grounded for want of an engine. A long-term engine lease runs for years and provides a permanent spare. <span class="cite">Owner: Part II; boxed in Part I §6.</span></dd>
<dt>Engine removal forecast</dt>
<dd>The engine removal forecast is an airline's rolling schedule of each installed engine's projected date of removal for a shop visit. Each engine's date is set by whichever of its limits is reached first: remaining exhaust-gas-temperature margin (Part I §5), remaining life-limited-part cycles (Part I §4), or a hard inspection. <span class="cite">Owner: Part III §22.</span></dd>
<dt>Engine sale / outright module sale / exchange</dt>
<dd>The segment's three product transactions. An engine sale books the whole-engine price as revenue. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VII §47.</span></dd>
<dt>Engine shop</dt>
<dd>An engine shop is a facility holding a regulatory repair-station approval (§9) and, in practice, the manufacturer's technical data and tooling. It disassembles engines on stands, cleans and inspects every piece, replaces or repairs what is out of limits, reassembles the engine and runs it in a test cell before release. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IV; boxed in Part I §6.</span></dd>
<dt>Engine Shop Manual (ESM)</dt>
<dd>The Engine Shop Manual is the manufacturer's manual defining how the engine is disassembled, inspected, repaired and rebuilt, including the limits a part must meet to stay in service. Regulators treat it as the reference: an Australian airworthiness directive notes that the "CFM56-7B ESM contains instructions for calculating remaining life of each engine stationary part within certain time frames and thresholds". <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §9.</span></dd>
<dt>Engine stand</dt>
<dd>An engine stand is the wheeled fixture that holds a roughly 2.5-tonne engine, or a module, while it is worked on, stored or transported. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IV §27.</span></dd>
<dt>EngineWise</dt>
<dd>EngineWise is Pratt &amp; Whitney's aftermarket services brand, under which IAE's network performs V2500 shop visits. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IV; boxed in Part I §10.</span></dd>
<dt>Equity commitment / hard cap</dt>
<dd>An equity commitment is an investor's promise to fund capital when the vehicle calls it; capital is drawn as assets are bought, not on signing. A hard cap is the maximum amount of commitments the sponsor will accept. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §61.</span></dd>
<dt>Equity method (Equity-method investment; equity pick-up; equity in earnings of unconsolidated entities)</dt>
<dd>An accounting method for an investment over which the investor has significant influence but not control. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §64.</span></dd>
<dt>Equity-method investment in Aviation Leasing</dt>
<dd>The unnamed investment through which the Aviation Leasing segment "leases and sells" assets, in the FY2025 10-K's segment description. Its counterparty, FTAI's ownership percentage, the assets inside it and the income it has contributed by year were not retrieved. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §64.</span></dd>
<dt>Escalation (spare parts and LLPs) (LLP escalation; catalogue escalation)</dt>
<dd>Escalation is the manufacturer's annual increase in catalogue list prices. Trade press put new spare-parts price increases at around 10% a year in 2022–23 (a double-digit increase on 1 November 2022 and a high-single-digit increase in August 2023), and Safran's chief executive described a rule of pricing "above inflation by 3 to 4 points". <span class="cite">Owner: Part IV; boxed in Part I §4.</span></dd>
<dt>Exchange fee / core (exchange sense)</dt>
<dd>The exchange fee is the cash price a customer pays to receive a serviceable module in return for an unserviceable one. The returned core is the unserviceable module the customer hands over, which has value to the exchanger as feedstock. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VII §47.</span></dd>
<dt>Exhaust-gas temperature (EGT)</dt>
<dd>Exhaust-gas temperature is the temperature of the gas leaving the turbine, measured by probes behind the low-pressure turbine. Every engine has a certified EGT red line that it may not exceed. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §5.</span></dd>
<dt>External management / manager / management agreement / management fee / incentive allocation / Master GP</dt>
<dd>External management is a structure in which a listed company has no staff of its own. A separate firm, the manager, supplies the executives, the employees and the investment process under a management agreement, and is paid a management fee, typically a percentage of the company's equity or assets. <span class="cite">Owner: Part X §76.</span></dd>
<dt>FAA Form 8110-3 (8110-3; Statement of Compliance with Airworthiness Standards)</dt>
<dd>Form 8110-3 is the document on which a Designated Engineering Representative records an approval, or a recommendation for approval, of engineering data, including the data for a repair or alteration. The designee keeps the original and sends copies to the FAA's managing office and to the owner, operator or repair station that requested the approval. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VI §40.</span></dd>
<dt>FAA Form 8130-3 (8130-3; Airworthiness Approval Tag; Authorized Release Certificate)</dt>
<dd>Form 8130-3 is the US tag. For a new part it states that the part, produced under an FAA production approval, conforms to its design and is in a condition for safe operation; for a used part it is the maintenance release by an FAA-approved repair station. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §9.</span></dd>
<dt>Fan frame (Fan frame sub-module)</dt>
<dd>The fan frame is the structural front assembly of the engine, built from four main pieces: the containment case that would catch a released fan blade, the outlet guide vanes that straighten the fan's airflow, the frame itself, and the housing for the radial shaft that drives the gearbox. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §3.</span></dd>
<dt>Feedstock</dt>
<dd>Feedstock is the trade's word for the aircraft and engines bought, or taken on consignment, to be torn down for parts. Later Parts extend it to the unserviceable module a customer returns in an exchange and to engine inventory held for module production, and say so when they do. <span class="cite">Owner: Part V; boxed in Part I §3.</span></dd>
<dt>Finance lease / sales-type lease</dt>
<dd>A finance lease transfers substantially all the economic risks and rewards of owning the asset to the lessee, so that in substance it is a loan secured on the asset rather than a rental. <span class="cite">Owner: Part II §12.</span></dd>
<dt>First run / mature run</dt>
<dd>The first run is the interval from new to the first shop visit. Mature runs are the later intervals, which are shorter because repaired hardware does not match new hardware and because operators plan them around the life-limit clock. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §6.</span></dd>
<dt>Fixed-to-floating / fixed-rate reset preferred (Reset preferred)</dt>
<dd>Two coupon designs appear. A fixed-to-floating preferred pays a fixed coupon until its first call date and then a floating rate, a reference rate plus a fixed spread, reset each quarter (Series A 8.25%, redeemed October 2024; Series B 8.00%, outstanding). <span class="cite">Owner: Part X §82.</span></dd>
<dt>Fleet maturity</dt>
<dd>Fleet maturity is the distribution of an installed base by the number of shop visits each engine has had: none, one, or more. GE reported in June 2019 that 57% of CFM56 engines had not had a first shop visit and 21% had had one; Safran reported in 2024 that about 70% had seen zero or one. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IV §26.</span></dd>
<dt>Fleet states: in service / parked (stored) / retired (withdrawn) (Fleet in service)</dt>
<dd>An aircraft in service is assigned to an operator and flying, or in short-term maintenance. A parked or stored aircraft is not flying but has not been formally withdrawn; an aircraft parked for more than about 90 days usually counts as stored, and a stored aircraft can return to service or go on to retirement. <span class="cite">Owner: Part III §18.</span></dd>
<dt>FLIGHT DECK</dt>
<dd>FLIGHT DECK is the name GE Aerospace gives its proprietary operating system for running its factories and shops. Its FY2025 annual report credits it with increasing material input from priority suppliers by more than 40% year on year, enabling a 26% rise in services revenue, and with the turnaround improvement quoted above. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IV §27.</span></dd>
<dt>Forensic accounting</dt>
<dd>Investigative accounting that tests transactions and records for misstatement, as distinct from the recurring audit of financial statements. The Audit Committee's advisors were described as "independent legal and forensic accounting advisors" in the February 2025 announcement and as "outside counsel and forensic accountants" in the FY2025 10-K. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VII §54.</span></dd>
<dt>Form 10-K / 10-Q / 8-K / DEF 14A / DEFM14A / S-4 (SEC forms)</dt>
<dd>A Form 10-K is a US-listed company's audited annual report. A Form 10-Q is its unaudited quarterly report. <span class="cite">Owner: Part X §75.</span></dd>
<dt>Form 20-F / Form 6-K</dt>
<dd>Form 20-F is the annual report a foreign private issuer files with the SEC in place of a Form 10-K, and Form 6-K is the form on which it furnishes interim results and other reports in place of 10-Qs and 8-Ks. AerCap, incorporated in the Netherlands and listed on the NYSE, files on these forms. <span class="cite">Owner: Part XI §87.</span></dd>
<dt>Fortress / FIG LLC / FTAI Infrastructure (Former Manager; FIP)</dt>
<dd>Fortress Investment Group is the alternative asset manager that sponsored FTAI's 2015 listing. FIG LLC, its subsidiary, was FTAI's external manager until 28 May 2024 and is called the "Former Manager" in the filings since. <span class="cite">Owner: Part X §76.</span></dd>
<dt>Frame (heavy-duty) turbine (F-, H-, J-class)</dt>
<dd>A frame turbine is a gas turbine designed from the start to sit on a foundation and make power, typically 50 to 600 MW per unit, heavy, and built in a factory for a delivery slot years ahead. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §68.</span></dd>
<dt>Free power turbine (FPT)</dt>
<dd>A free power turbine is an output turbine on its own shaft, not mechanically connected to the gas generator; it is driven only by the gas stream, which the patent calls "aerodynamically coupled". Because the two shafts are independent, the gas generator can speed up and slow down with load while the output shaft holds the fixed speed a generator needs. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §68.</span></dd>
<dt>Freighter conversion (P2F (passenger-to-freighter); conversion STC)</dt>
<dd>Freighter conversion is the structural modification of a used passenger airframe into a cargo aircraft: a main-deck cargo door, a strengthened floor, a rigid barrier between the cockpit and the hold, and a cargo loading system. It extends the airframe's revenue life by 10 to 15 years at low cycles a year. <span class="cite">Owner: Part II §15.</span></dd>
<dt>FTAI–Chromalloy joint venture (Chromalloy JV; PMA JV)</dt>
<dd>FTAI's FY2025 annual report says only that the company "develops and manufactures Parts Manufacturer Approval ('PMA') parts through a joint venture", as part of a "proprietary portfolio of products that enables it to provide cost savings and flexibility to airline, lessor, and maintenance, repair, and operations customers"; the partner, the ownership split, the accounting treatment, the revenue and the savings are not in the filing text retrieved. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VI §43.</span></dd>
<dt>Full-life value (Full life)</dt>
<dd>Full-life value is the value with every maintenance item fresh and every life-limited part at zero cycles: "full-life engines are brand new or in recently restored condition". It equals the half-life value plus half the cost of a full restoration and a full set of life-limited parts. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §8.</span></dd>
<dt>Gain on sale of leased equipment / to the 2025 Partnership</dt>
<dd>The sale price of an asset sold from the leasing portfolio minus its net book value. FTAI recorded $46.4M of such gains on sales to the 2025 Partnership in FY2025; the three quarterly gains disclosed for 2025 sum to $50.1M for nine months, which is a tension (§60.2). <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §60.</span></dd>
<dt>Gas turbine / gas generator</dt>
<dd>A gas turbine is a rotating engine that compresses air, burns fuel in the compressed air and expands the hot gas through turbine stages. The compressor, the combustor and the turbine that drives the compressor together form the gas generator: the assembly whose only product is hot, pressurised gas. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §68.</span></dd>
<dt>GE segment profit</dt>
<dd>GE segment profit is GE Aerospace's own segment-level profit measure, which excludes certain corporate and non-operating items. For Commercial Engines &amp; Services it was $8,861M on $33,314M in 2025, a 26.6% margin. <span class="cite">Owner: Part XI §86.</span></dd>
<dt>Gearbox (genset)</dt>
<dd>A genset gearbox is the speed reducer between the turbine's output shaft and the generator. A generator synchronised to a 60 Hz grid must turn at 3,600 rpm with two poles or 1,800 rpm with four, and aeroderivative output shafts commonly spin faster, so "an external gearbox is typically required to obtain the proper input speed to a generator". <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §68.</span></dd>
<dt>Geared turbofan (GTF; PW1100G (A320neo); PW1500G, PW1900G)</dt>
<dd>The geared turbofan is Pratt &amp; Whitney's new-generation engine. A reduction gearbox between the fan and the low-pressure turbine lets each turn at its own best speed, which the CFM56's direct-drive design does not allow. <span class="cite">Owner: Part III §20.</span></dd>
<dt>Generator set (Genset)</dt>
<dd>A generator set is the complete unit that turns fuel into electricity: the prime mover (a gas turbine or a piston engine), the output turbine where there is one, a gearbox if the shaft speed must be changed, the electrical generator itself, and the surrounding fuel, air-intake, exhaust, lubrication, fire-protection and control systems, assembled and tested as one product. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §68.</span></dd>
<dt>Glider</dt>
<dd>A glider is a completed airframe parked at the factory awaiting engines. Airbus had about 60 at the end of the first half of 2025 and 32 at the end of the third quarter, and its chief executive said the number would reach zero by year-end. <span class="cite">Owner: Part III §21.</span></dd>
<dt>Global Replacement Ratio</dt>
<dd>The Global Replacement Ratio is the number of deliveries needed to replace retirements, expressed as a share of total deliveries over a period. Cirium put it at 46.1% for 2025 on 2015–2024 data: fewer than half of new deliveries were replacing a retired aircraft, and the rest were growth. <span class="cite">Owner: Part III §19.</span></dd>
<dt>GMF AeroAsia / EgyptAir partnerships (Capacity partnerships (Indonesia, Egypt))</dt>
<dd>Capacity partnerships referenced on the Q2 2026 call with Garuda Indonesia's MRO arm and with EgyptAir Maintenance &amp; Engineering, adding module capacity in Indonesia and Egypt. Their terms, capacity and economics were not retrieved. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VII.</span></dd>
<dt>Green time / green-time engine / green-time lease</dt>
<dd>Green time is the remaining usable life on an engine or module before its next mandatory shop visit, set by whichever runs out first: the life-limited part with the fewest cycles remaining, the EGT margin, or a hard inspection or directive. <span class="cite">Owner: Part II; boxed in Part I §8.</span></dd>
<dt>Gross leasing yield</dt>
<dd>Gross leasing yield is lease income for a period divided by the average leasing equipment at cost over the period, or at net book value (cost less accumulated depreciation). <span class="cite">Owner: Part II §14.</span></dd>
<dt>Gross margin (AerSale Asset Management Solutions) (AMS gross margin)</dt>
<dd>AerSale's AMS gross margin is the segment's gross profit divided by its sales: $74.1M on $211.6M in 2025, 35.0% (derived in the research), against $82.5M on $215.5M in 2024, 38.3%. The filing attributes the fall to "lower margin on USM sales resulting from changes in the product mix" and reports the aircraft line within AMS at 33.2% against 34.8%. <span class="cite">Owner: Part XI §86.</span></dd>
<dt>GTF Advantage</dt>
<dd>The GTF Advantage is Pratt &amp; Whitney's upgraded PW1100G, certified by EASA in 2026 for entry into service later in 2026. It is the fix for the fleet going forward; the discs already in service are dealt with by the inspection programme. <span class="cite">Owner: Part III §20.</span></dd>
<dt>GTF powder-metal issue (Powder metal)</dt>
<dd>Certain turbine discs in the GTF are made by a powder-metallurgy route, in which metal powder is consolidated into a disc. Powder produced over a period of years was found to be contaminated. <span class="cite">Owner: Part III §20.</span></dd>
<dt>Guidance</dt>
<dd>Guidance is management's published forward target for a financial measure. FTAI gives it in Adjusted EBITDA from its business segments, by segment and in total, excluding Corporate and Other, and in modules produced by Aerospace Products. <span class="cite">Owner: Part X §84.</span></dd>
<dt>Half-life (base) value (Half life; HLBV)</dt>
<dd>Half-life is an appraisal convention, not a physical midpoint. In ISTAT's words it is "a standard appraisal industry term to indicate that no value adjustment has been made for the actual maintenance status ... the assumption being that the airframe, engines (modules &amp; LLPs), landing gear, and other major maintenance events are in half-life status. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §8.</span></dd>
<dt>Half-life trade</dt>
<dd>The half-life trade is this primer's term, not the sources', for the trade in engines, modules and life-limited parts that are part-way through their lives and priced on the life they have left: a part-worn engine leased for its remaining months or torn down now; a module valued on the remaining cycles of the life-limited parts inside it; a used disk priced at its life fraction times list price less a discount (§34). <span class="cite">Owner: Part V §32.</span></dd>
<dt>Hard-time part (HT part)</dt>
<dd>A hard-time part is one with a fixed overhaul or replacement interval, as distinct from an on-condition part that is replaced when inspection finds it worn. It needs complete operating-history records and evidence of compliance with every applicable Airworthiness Directive and Service Bulletin. <span class="cite">Owner: Part V §31.</span></dd>
<dt>Heat rate (Btu/kWh)</dt>
<dd>The heat rate is the fuel energy a plant needs to make one kilowatt-hour of electricity, in Btu per kWh. One kWh is 3,412 Btu of energy, so a plant's efficiency is 3,412 divided by its heat rate. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §68.</span></dd>
<dt>HEICO Flight Support Group (HEI; FSG)</dt>
<dd>The Flight Support Group is the larger of HEICO Corporation's two operating groups and the largest independent PMA business. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VI §41.</span></dd>
<dt>High-pressure compressor (HPC)</dt>
<dd>The high-pressure compressor is the nine-stage compressor on the high-pressure spool, driven by the high-pressure turbine behind it. It raises the core airflow to the pressure at which fuel can be burned efficiently. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §3.</span></dd>
<dt>High-pressure turbine (HPT)</dt>
<dd>The high-pressure turbine is the single-stage turbine immediately behind the combustor. It takes power from the hottest gas in the engine and uses it to drive the high-pressure compressor. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §3.</span></dd>
<dt>History timeline</dt>
<dd>The dated sequence of corporate events that Part X assembles: the 2015 IPO of Fortress Transportation and Infrastructure Investors, the 2022 spin-off and redomiciliation, the 2023 QuickTurn and 2024 Montréal acquisitions, the 2024 internalization, the launch of the Strategic Capital Initiative and of FTAI Power, the 2025 short report, the 2026 CFM materials agreement, the J&amp;F order, the 2026 SPV warehouse, the $500M buyback authorisation and the WestJet purchase of 28 September 2026. Part X §75 carries the table with each date and source. <span class="cite">Owner: Part X.</span></dd>
<dt>Hospital (quick-turn) visit (Quick-turn; QTSV)</dt>
<dd>A hospital or quick-turn visit fixes one thing and returns the engine: a borescope finding, a damaged fan blade, a module exchange, an accessory change. Trade press reports that such visits "are being used as an alternative to avoid substantial financial and time investments into full-performance shop visits", at about 45 days' turnaround. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VII; boxed in Part I §6.</span></dd>
<dt>Hot section</dt>
<dd>The hot section is the combustor, the high-pressure turbine blades and vanes (the vanes are also called nozzles; the stage-1 vane is the HPT nozzle), and the adjacent parts that combustion gas touches directly. It is where margin is lost and where most of it is recovered. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §5.</span></dd>
<dt>Hurdle / preferred return</dt>
<dd>The return limited partners receive on their capital before the sponsor shares in profit beyond its own pro-rata interest; an 8% annual preferred return is a common private-fund convention, used here only as an illustration in §66. FTAI's filings do not disclose whether the 2025 Partnership has one. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §63.</span></dd>
<dt>Hydro-mechanical unit (HMU)</dt>
<dd>The hydro-mechanical unit is the engine's fuel-metering unit, the accessory that sets fuel flow under the engine control's command. It is a high-value engine accessory and, with CFM56 fan disks and structural engine parts, one of the most searched-for teardown items in one 2026 consultancy report. <span class="cite">Owner: Part V §32.</span></dd>
<dt>Hyperscaler</dt>
<dd>A hyperscaler is a very large cloud-computing operator that builds and runs its own data centers at a scale of gigawatts. The research's sources describe the J&amp;F customer two ways: FTAI's release of 22 July 2026 says "a leading international cloud service provider", and summaries of the second-quarter 2026 call say "a U.S. hyperscaler". <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §70.</span></dd>
<dt>Identicality (with / without a licensing agreement)</dt>
<dd>Identicality is the PMA basis in which the applicant shows that its design is identical to the type-certificate holder's in every respect that matters. Without a licence, the applicant usually holds the manufacturer's drawings legitimately, for example as a former supplier; FAA Notice 8110.55 of 1996 set the policy. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VI §37.</span></dd>
<dt>Independent MRO / MRO (Maintenance, Repair and Overhaul)</dt>
<dd>MRO is the industry's generic term for maintenance, repair and overhaul work and for the companies that do it. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IV; boxed in Part I §6.</span></dd>
<dt>Induction / induction slot (Slot)</dt>
<dd>Induction is the acceptance of an engine into work at a shop, the point from which turnaround time is counted. An induction slot is the date a shop commits to start a given engine. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IV §27.</span></dd>
<dt>Installed base</dt>
<dd>The installed base is the population of engines fitted to aircraft in the operating fleet. Published figures for the CFM56 "in service" range from about 23,000 to about 24,000 and may include spare and stored engines as well as installed ones, since none of the sources states its basis. <span class="cite">Owner: Part III; boxed in Part I §2.</span></dd>
<dt>Interconnection agreement / interconnection queue</dt>
<dd>An interconnection agreement is the contract between a generator or a large customer and the utility or grid operator that governs its connection to the grid. The interconnection queue is the waiting list of projects seeking such agreements, which in the United States runs to years. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §73.</span></dd>
<dt>Internalization</dt>
<dd>Internalization is the termination of an external management agreement, the hiring of the manager's people directly by the company, and a payment to the manager for the fee stream it gives up. <span class="cite">Owner: Part X §76.</span></dd>
<dt>Inventory (Module Factory working capital)</dt>
<dd>Serviceable modules and parts awaiting exchange or sale, plus unserviceable engines, modules and parts in work: $1,364.3M net at 31 March 2026. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VII §53.</span></dd>
<dt>ISTAT (International Society of Transport Aircraft Trading)</dt>
<dd>ISTAT, the International Society of Transport Aircraft Trading, is the trade body whose definitions standardise appraisal terms such as half-life and full-life. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §8.</span></dd>
<dt>Jereh Group (Yantai Jereh Oilfield Services Group (SZSE 002353))</dt>
<dd>Jereh is a Chinese maker of oilfield equipment listed on the Shenzhen Stock Exchange (SZSE) under code 002353: drilling and oil-and-gas field engineering equipment, equipment maintenance and parts, oilfield engineering services, natural-gas compressors and gas-turbine generator sets. Its gas-turbine genset research began in 2018, and "in 2020, the first 6MW gas turbine genset in China was successfully applied in the well site". <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §70.</span></dd>
<dt>J&amp;F Power Systems LLC (J&amp;F)</dt>
<dd>J&amp;F Power Systems is the entity through which Mod-1 is packaged and sold. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §70.</span></dd>
<dt>LEAP (LEAP-1A / LEAP-1B)</dt>
<dd>The LEAP is CFM's new-generation engine: the LEAP-1A is one of two options on the Airbus A320neo family, and the LEAP-1B is the sole engine of the Boeing 737 MAX (the re-engined 737). About 5,500 were in service at the date of one trade-press piece, with about 50% under long-term service agreements against about 15% of the CFM56 fleet. <span class="cite">Owner: Part III; boxed in Part I §10.</span></dd>
<dt>Lease extension / extension share</dt>
<dd>A lease extension renews an expiring operating lease with the incumbent airline instead of taking the aircraft back for transition, sale or part-out. The extension share is the proportion of scheduled lease expiries that are extended rather than returned. <span class="cite">Owner: Part III §22.</span></dd>
<dt>Lease income / maintenance revenue / asset sales revenue</dt>
<dd>The Aviation Leasing segment's three income-statement lines. Lease income is rent from operating leases of aircraft and engines ($235.2M for 2025). <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §58.</span></dd>
<dt>Lease rate (Rent)</dt>
<dd>The lease rate is the periodic rent, quoted per month. <span class="cite">Owner: Part II §12.</span></dd>
<dt>Lease rate factor (LRF)</dt>
<dd>The lease rate factor is the monthly rent divided by the asset's current market value, expressed as a percentage. An LRF of 1.0% means annual rent is 12% of value. <span class="cite">Owner: Part II §12.</span></dd>
<dt>Lease transition</dt>
<dd>A lease transition is the move of an aircraft from one lessee to the next. Between the two leases the lessor pays for reconfiguration of the cabin, for the maintenance needed to meet the next lessee's delivery condition, and for the downtime while the aircraft earns no rent. <span class="cite">Owner: Part III §19.</span></dd>
<dt>Lease-attached trading</dt>
<dd>Lease-attached trading is buying an aircraft from another lessor with the existing lease transferred to the buyer, so that the buyer acquires a rent stream and a return-condition obligation rather than an empty aircraft. The lease is what makes the asset financeable: lenders and securitization investors underwrite contracted rent from a known airline. <span class="cite">Owner: Part II §12.</span></dd>
<dt>Lessor / lessee</dt>
<dd>The lessor is the owner that rents out an aircraft or engine; the lessee is the operator that rents it. Plain words that need no box; Part II §12 uses them from its first paragraph. <span class="cite">Owner: Part II.</span></dd>
<dt>Letter of intent (LOI)</dt>
<dd>A signed agreement to transact that is non-binding, or binding only on conditions, ahead of a definitive purchase contract. At the hard-cap date the 2025 Partnership had "190 aircraft closed or under LOI", of which 101 had closed for $1.4B and the rest were under contract for $2.1B. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §62.</span></dd>
<dt>LHV / HHV (Lower / higher heating value)</dt>
<dd>A fuel's energy content can be stated on two bases. The higher heating value counts the heat recovered by condensing the water vapour in the exhaust; the lower heating value does not. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §68.</span></dd>
<dt>Licensed / joint-venture shop</dt>
<dd>A licensed shop is an engine shop that performs CFM56 work under a licence from the manufacturer; a joint-venture shop is one in which the manufacturer holds equity, often alongside an airline. Both operate with guaranteed access to the manufacturer's parts and technical data, and both are counted as part of the OEM network rather than as independents. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IV §25.</span></dd>
<dt>Life-expired</dt>
<dd>A life-limited part is life-expired when it has reached the cycle limit the manufacturer certified for it. It has no used-material value, however good its condition, and it must be mutilated so that it cannot re-enter the supply chain. <span class="cite">Owner: Part V §31.</span></dd>
<dt>Life-limited part (LLP)</dt>
<dd>A life-limited part is a rotating or pressure-bearing part (a disk, a spool, a shaft, certain seals) whose failure the engine case cannot be designed to contain. It is certified for a fixed number of flight cycles and must be removed and scrapped at that limit regardless of its condition. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §4.</span></dd>
<dt>Limited partner / general partner (LP / GP)</dt>
<dd>A limited partner is an investor who commits capital to a partnership, has limited liability and takes no part in managing it. A general partner manages the partnership and bears liability. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §61.</span></dd>
<dt>Line-replaceable unit / quick-engine-change kit (LRU / QEC)</dt>
<dd>A line-replaceable unit is an accessory that can be changed on the flight line without opening the engine. The quick-engine-change kit is the set of mounts, plumbing and accessories that dresses a bare engine for installation on a particular aircraft. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §6.</span></dd>
<dt>LLP stack (Full LLP set; shipset (2008 usage))</dt>
<dd>Another name for the full set of life-limited parts in an engine, which Part I calls the full LLP set; its remaining life largely sets a used engine's value. A 2008 price table calls the same thing a shipset. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I.</span></dd>
<dt>LLP stagger</dt>
<dd>The mismatch of remaining lives across an engine's life-limited parts: replacing only the limiting part brings the next LLP-forced removal sooner. It is the same mechanism Part I §4 treats as stub life. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I.</span></dd>
<dt>Loan-to-cost</dt>
<dd>A vehicle's debt divided by its total capital (equity plus debt). It is distinct from loan-to-value, which divides debt by the appraised value of the assets (Part II §17). <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §61.</span></dd>
<dt>Loan-to-value (LTV)</dt>
<dd>Loan-to-value is the note balance divided by the appraised value of the assets in the pool. The senior notes might be sized to 60–70% of appraised value and the senior and mezzanine notes together to 75–80%, leaving equity of 20–25%; those ranges are illustrative, because the two deals the research retrieved did not return their loan-to-value ratios (D6 U6). <span class="cite">Owner: Part II §17.</span></dd>
<dt>Lockheed Martin Commercial Engine Solutions (LMCES; Montréal)</dt>
<dd>Lockheed Martin Commercial Engine Solutions (LMCES) was the Montréal engine shop that FTAI acquired from Lockheed Martin Canada, closing on 9 September 2024 for total consideration of $170.0 million; the shop is about 500,000 square feet in the annual report and 526,000 in the closing release. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VII; boxed in Part IV §25.</span></dd>
<dt>Long-term service agreement (LTSA; LTA (D9); rate-per-flight-hour (RPFH); flight-hour agreement)</dt>
<dd>A long-term service agreement is a multi-year contract under which the operator pays a fixed amount per engine flight hour and the manufacturer, or a shop, commits to perform or pay for the engine's shop visits. The provider takes the volume and cost risk and in return controls the workscope, the parts sourcing and the choice of shop. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IV §28.</span></dd>
<dt>Low-pressure turbine (LPT)</dt>
<dd>The low-pressure turbine is the four-stage turbine at the rear of the engine. It takes the remaining energy from the gas after the high-pressure turbine and drives the fan and booster through the long low-pressure shaft. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §3.</span></dd>
<dt>LP spool / HP spool; two-shaft (two-spool) design (LP; HP)</dt>
<dd>A spool is a rotating assembly of compressor and turbine stages on one shaft. The CFM56 has two, one inside the other. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §1.</span></dd>
<dt>Maintenance cliff</dt>
<dd>The maintenance cliff is the point at which the next heavy maintenance event, an engine shop visit, a life-limited-part replacement or a heavy airframe check, costs more than the aircraft is worth kept. An owner at the cliff retires, converts or sells the aircraft rather than pay for the event. <span class="cite">Owner: Part III §19.</span></dd>
<dt>Maintenance, Repair and Exchange (MRE)</dt>
<dd>MRE is FTAI's name for its offer to engine owners, chosen as a contrast with the industry's MRO. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VII §45.</span></dd>
<dt>Maintenance reserves (Supplemental rent; maintenance rents (AerCap))</dt>
<dd>Maintenance reserves are periodic payments the lessee makes to the lessor in addition to basic rent, "calculated with reference to the utilization of airframes, engines and other major life-limited components during the lease". <span class="cite">Owner: Part II §12.</span></dd>
<dt>Maintenance rights</dt>
<dd>Maintenance rights is a line in Willis Lease's lease portfolio ($30.6M at 31 Dec 2025; $27.0M at 30 Sep 2025) that the research reports without a filing definition. <span class="cite">Owner: Part XI §87.</span></dd>
<dt>Maintenance-adjusted value (Maintenance adjustment)</dt>
<dd>Maintenance-adjusted value is "the fair market value of an Aircraft or Engine based on half-life values as adjusted by the relevant Aircraft Appraiser for the actual maintenance conditions and specifications". For each item the adjustment is (actual remaining fraction − 0.5) multiplied by the cost to restore that item, summed module by module. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §8.</span></dd>
<dt>Major module / sub-module</dt>
<dd>The manufacturer's shop manual arranges the engine as a hierarchy. The major modules (the fan and booster, the core, the low-pressure turbine, and the accessory gearbox) are each built from numbered sub-modules. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §3.</span></dd>
<dt>Major repair</dt>
<dd>A major repair is a repair the manufacturer's manual does not cover, or a repair of a part the manual says must be scrapped, that does not qualify as minor. Major repairs "must be accomplished in accordance with technical data approved by the Administrator", meaning the FAA or someone acting under its delegation. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VI §40.</span></dd>
<dt>Management fee / incentive fee / sales fee</dt>
<dd>Three fee types in third-party-capital vehicles: a management or servicing fee on capital committed, assets managed or rents collected; an incentive fee for performance; and a sales fee on disposal proceeds. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §63.</span></dd>
<dt>Margin pool</dt>
<dd>The margin pool is this primer's term for how the profit earned in keeping an engine type flying is distributed across the links of the chain: the manufacturer's new parts and services, the shops' labour and test, the component repairers, the makers of approved alternative parts, the traders in used material and teardown, the lessors of engines and aircraft, and the servicers of third-party capital. <span class="cite">Owner: Part XI §86.</span></dd>
<dt>Market value</dt>
<dd>Base value is an appraiser's opinion of an asset's underlying economic value in an open, unrestricted, stable market with balanced supply and demand. Market value is the appraiser's opinion of the most likely trading price in the actual current market between willing, informed parties; it sits above base value in a tight market and below it in a soft one. <span class="cite">Owner: Part II; boxed in Part I §8.</span></dd>
<dt>Master supply agreement / purchase order / milestone payments / performance adjustment mechanism</dt>
<dd>A master supply agreement is a framework contract that fixes terms under which a buyer may place binding orders over its life; the orders themselves are purchase orders. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §70.</span></dd>
<dt>Material solutions</dt>
<dd>Material solutions is the generic trade phrase for a shop's or a manufacturer's package of used serviceable material, repaired parts and approved alternative parts assembled to reduce the new-parts bill of a shop visit. The whole commercial logic of a shop that is not the manufacturer sits on this line, since material is 60–70% of a visit. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IV §28.</span></dd>
<dt>Mid-life narrowbody (Mid-life)</dt>
<dd>A mid-life narrowbody is a 737NG or A320ceo roughly 10 to 18 years old, or more loosely one in the middle of a 25-to-30-year economic life, when an increasing share of the aircraft's value sits in its engines and those engines are approaching or past a shop visit. <span class="cite">Owner: Part II; boxed in Part I §4.</span></dd>
<dt>Mini-pack (Records mini-pack)</dt>
<dd>A mini-pack is an engine records summary that lists each life-limited part with its certified limit and the cycles it has consumed. Traders publish them so that a buyer can see the engine's remaining life part by part before asking for the full records. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §4.</span></dd>
<dt>Mobile genset</dt>
<dd>A mobile generator set is one built onto a road trailer or a skid so that it can be driven to a site, connected to a gas supply and to the customer's switchgear, commissioned in days or weeks, and later moved. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §68.</span></dd>
<dt>Mod-1 (FTAI Mod-1; CFM56 aeroderivative)</dt>
<dd>Mod-1 is FTAI Power's 25 MW trailer-mounted natural-gas generator set built around a CFM56 core. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §69.</span></dd>
<dt>Module</dt>
<dd>A module is a section of the engine designed to be removed and replaced as a unit. It is bolted to its neighbours at flanges, and it carries its own part number, its own serial number and its own maintenance record. <span class="cite">Owner: Part I §3.</span></dd>
<dt>Module as unit of trade</dt>
<dd>A serviceable module from a dismantled engine is valued like a whole engine in miniature: its serviceable parts at a discount to list, plus the remaining life of the life-limited parts inside it, less the repair needed to make it serviceable. <span class="cite">Owner: Part V; boxed in Part I §8.</span></dd>
<dt>Module exchange / engine exchange</dt>
<dd>A module exchange separates the engine at the major-module flanges, removes a time-expired or damaged module and installs a serviceable one from a pool, so the aircraft returns to service in days to weeks instead of months. The incoming unserviceable module becomes the provider's feedstock. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VII; boxed in Part I §3.</span></dd>
<dt>Module Factory</dt>
<dd>The Module Factory is FTAI's name for the CFM56-7B and -5B modular repair and refurbishment programme quoted above from its FY2025 annual report, based at its Montréal shop. Functionally it is a production-line approach that builds and holds an inventory of serviceable modules for exchange. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VII; boxed in Part I §3.</span></dd>
<dt>Module (FTAI Power usage)</dt>
<dd>In this Part a module is counted as FTAI's releases count it: three per engine, the fan, the core and the low-pressure turbine, which is the convention evidenced by "450 modules (150 engines)" and "1,800 modules (600 engines)" in FTAI's release of 26 February 2025. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §69.</span></dd>
<dt>Modules produced / refurbished (count)</dt>
<dd>FTAI's quarterly count of CFM56 modules its Module Factory has refurbished and made serviceable: 184 in the second quarter of 2025, 228 in the fourth, 757 for 2025 against a target of 750, 296 in the second quarter of 2026 and 566 for the first half. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VII §49.</span></dd>
<dt>MRE Contract revenue</dt>
<dd>The income-statement line, new in FY2025, for FTAI's sales of CFM56-5B, CFM56-7B and V2500 engines and related modules to the special-purpose vehicles of the 2025 Partnership under the MRE Contract: $335.8M for 2025, $221.2M in the first quarter of 2026, $182.8M in the second (against $69.6M a year earlier) and $404.0M for the first half of 2026 (against $170.2M). <span class="cite">Owner: Part VII §48.</span></dd>
<dt>MRO market / engine MRO</dt>
<dd>MRO is maintenance, repair and overhaul, the generic term for aircraft and engine upkeep (Part IV §25). The MRO market is the dollar value of that spending in a year, and engine MRO is the engine share of it. <span class="cite">Owner: Part III §23.</span></dd>
<dt>Muddy Waters report / short report / short seller</dt>
<dd>A short seller is an investor positioned to profit if a share price falls; a short report is research such an investor publishes after taking that position. Muddy Waters Research published a report on FTAI on 15 January 2025. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VII §54.</span></dd>
<dt>Named executive officers and compensation (NEO; say-on-pay; CEO pay ratio; pay-versus-performance)</dt>
<dd>The named executive officers are the officers whose compensation a proxy statement must disclose in full, normally the chief executive, the chief financial officer and the next three highest-paid. <span class="cite">Owner: Part X §78.</span></dd>
<dt>Net book value (NBV)</dt>
<dd>Net book value is an asset's cost less accumulated depreciation and impairments. It is the denominator for a lessor's implied yield and the figure against which a gain on sale is measured. <span class="cite">Owner: Part XI §86.</span></dd>
<dt>Net debt / leverage ratio</dt>
<dd>Net debt is total debt less cash. A leverage ratio divides debt, or net debt, by a measure of annual earnings, usually a form of EBITDA. <span class="cite">Owner: Part X §82.</span></dd>
<dt>Non-incident statement</dt>
<dd>A non-incident statement is the seller's declaration that a part was not installed on an aircraft involved in an accident, a fire or an immersion. It is a standard element of a used-material documentation package, beside the removal tag, the teardown report and the certificate of conformance. <span class="cite">Owner: Part V §31.</span></dd>
<dt>Nonroad engine</dt>
<dd>A nonroad engine is a category in US air rules for engines that are mobile or temporary, generally meaning at one location for less than twelve months, and that are exempt from the permitting that applies to stationary sources. Whether trailer-mounted turbines at a data center qualify is contested. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §73.</span></dd>
<dt>NOx / selective catalytic reduction (SCR)</dt>
<dd>Nitrogen oxides are the pollutant that air permits limit most tightly for gas turbines. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IX §68.</span></dd>
<dt>OEM (Original equipment manufacturer)</dt>
<dd>The OEM is the company that holds the type certificate for the engine, meaning the regulator's approval of its design, and that built it. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IV; boxed in Part I §2.</span></dd>
<dt>OEM aftermarket model</dt>
<dd>The OEM aftermarket model is the way the engine's manufacturer earns from its installed base after delivery: spare parts sold at an escalating catalogue list price to every shop, plus branded services (shop visits, parts and repair packages, used material, repair management) and long-term agreements priced per flight hour. <span class="cite">Owner: Part IV §28.</span></dd>
<dt>OEM-only clause (No-PMA / no-DER clause; TCH-configuration clause)</dt>
<dd>An OEM-only clause is a lease return condition requiring that at redelivery the engine contain only parts produced by the type-certificate holder and only repairs from the type-certificate holder's manuals. A lessee that used PMA parts or DER repairs during the term must then either remove them at the last heavy check before return or pay compensation. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VI §42.</span></dd>
<dt>OneIM (One Investment Management)</dt>
<dd>An investment manager announced on 25 March 2025 as FTAI's partner "on the inaugural vehicle", in a release that described more than $4 billion of deployment for the vehicle. OneIM's commitment size, and whether its role is that of an anchor limited partner or a co-general partner, are not disclosed. <span class="cite">Owner: Part VIII §62.</span></dd>
<dt>On-lease</dt>
